Chroma Ate Inc.  | Asia Pacific
September  Sales  +22% MoM/
+206% YoY
In this report, we focus on Chroma's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for share price movement.
Details:
• September revenue was NT$6,860mn (22% MoM/+206% YoY), a record
high.
• 3Q26 revenue came in at  NT$17,926mn (+32% QoQ/+180% YoY),
significantly higher than  our estimate at NT$14,772mn (+9% QoQ/+130%
YoY) and consensus at NT$14,555mn (+8% QoQ/+127% YoY).

Our view:
• We believe that the strength in 3Q26 was mainly driven by the
semiconductor and photonics business, especially SL T, metrology,
transceiver/CPO testing, etc.
• We think that Chroma might start to ship FT handlers and ovens in small
volume from late 2026/early 2027, which represent new business
opportunities for the coming years.
• Stay OW.

Morgan Stanley Research 3
Valuation Methodology and Risks
Chroma Ate Inc. (2360.TW)
Base case, 45x 2027e P/E. We use P/E as our valuation methodology because we think it better
captures Chroma's earnings growth momentum. The stock traded at an average valuation of
~19x P/E during 2014-25 with an operating profit CAGR of 20%, implying a PEG of 1.0. We think
our target multiple of 45x is justified by the 61% operating profit CAGR we project for

2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected macro conditions
n Stronger semi and photonics and power testing demand
n Margin improvement via better product mix
n Better opex control
Risks to Downside
n Macro slowdown
n Softer-than-expected AI capex
n Slower progress in the advanced packaging capacity expansion
n Weaker consumer electronics and automobile related demand
n Margin contraction arising from lack of revenue scale and increasing opex

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

營收走勢圖

觀光事業  
八方雲集(2753.TT) 
台灣穩健成長，美國快餐新模式開啟新引擎

研究部預估八方雲集 2026 年 EPS 14.07 元(+12.2%YOY)，2027 年
15.05 元(+9.5%YOY)，對八方營運仍持正面看法，主要是考量：(1)下半
年肉品價格有所回落 ，成本壓力減輕， 加上公司嚴格控管美國地區費用，
下半年營業利益將能改善 ；(2)美國市場仍是公司中長期業績成長的主要
動能。儘管德州門市延後至今年 9 月才開幕，但快餐新模式初期單店營
收與營運效率均優於預期，有望改善美國事業營運業績。研究部給予 15
倍本益比，目標價 225 元，維持買進評等。

1. 雙品牌展店穩步推進，台灣營運維持成長 
八方雲集與梁社漢持續在台灣展店，截至2026 年 8 月底門市分別達
1,058 家及 260 家 ， 較去年底淨增25 家及 12 家 。 既有門市表現亦穩
健，1～8 月八方與梁社漢同店銷售分別成長約 5%及 6%。 未來公司將
持續開發科技園區、辦公大樓及封閉型商圈，並擴大 To Go 小型店
型，以提高市場滲透率。研究部預期台灣區 2026 年、2027 年營收將
分別成長 8.3%YoY 與 5.0%YoY。

2. 美國市場展店增加，營運有望改善 
美國事業目前仍處於快速擴張期 ，2025 年底總店數達 13 家，2026 年
上半年在加州新增 3 家門店，9 月底達拉斯開幕 ，之後德州還有 2 家
門店預計在年底前開出，預期至 2027 年將至少新增 5 家，屆時明年
底總家數有望達到 25 家。研究部預估美國區今明兩年營收都將有約
40%的增速，令2027 年美國事業虧損幅度收斂，並有望於 2028 年實
現年度損平。

3. 快餐新模式初步驗證成功，具跨區複製潛力 
達拉斯快餐新模式不僅提高出餐速度 ， 也可簡化人力與教育訓練 ， 初
期營運表現優於公司原先預期 。 公司計畫將此模式複製至美國新店 ，
甚至改造部分效率較低的加州門市，有望成為未來美國展店與改善
業績的重要模式。

一、營運概況：

八方雲集仍以台灣市場為公司主要營運重心 ， 美國市場則是逐步提高營收
占比。上半年品牌營收比重分別為：八方雲集 74.7%、梁社漢 24.8%、芳
珍 0.4%、丹堤 0.1%。

圖一、八方雲集 1H26 區域營收比重、品牌營收比重

資料來源：法說資料

台灣市場：雙品牌展店提速，數位點餐創造更多客流 
台灣市場為八方雲集最重要的營運與獲利支柱 。受惠於雙品牌規模經濟發
酵 與 中 央 工 廠 效 益 提 升， 台 灣 地 區 1H26 營收 上 升 至 35.60 億元
(+7.1%YoY)，獲利5.98 億元(+22.3%YoY)，獲利增速顯著優於營收。這波
成長動能延續到下半年。截至 2026 年 8 月底，主品牌「八方雲集」台灣
門市達 1,058 家，較去年底淨增 25 家； 「梁社漢排骨」達260 家，較去年
底淨增 12 家，其中加盟店占比已提升至 84%。

台灣市場仍是 八方雲集 主要營運
重心，上半年台灣區營收比重達
77.5%

八方雲集在台灣市場的餐飲品牌
以加盟店較多。海外市場則是以
直營店為主。

### Page 3

請參見末頁重要披露及免責聲明 3

此外，數位化轉型效益逐步顯現，線上點餐系統於加速導入後，有效縮短
尖峰時間的候餐期 ， 並提升門市整體服務量能。在今年尚未調漲售價的前
提下 ，1～8 月八方雲集與梁社漢的同店銷售增長率分別達 5%與 6%， 顯示
成長動能主要來自來客數與客單價的雙重提升，整體營運維持穩健。

圖三、八方雲集台灣區(年底)門店數量變化

美國市場：業務進軍德州，營收增速保持強勁 
美國市場自 2022 年 3 月以南加州為起點，設立加州中央工廠，並以直營
方式陸續展店， 已成為八方雲集目前最重要的海外擴張重心 ， 隨著新店持
續開出，營運規模正快速放大。在總門店家數由去年底的 13 家擴增至 16
家的帶動下，美國地區 1H26 營收則提升至 4.38 億元(+51.7%YoY)，展現
強勁的成長動能。

位於德州的達拉斯首店於 9 月正式開幕，將全美營運門市推升至 17 家。
目前德州另有 2 家門市正在裝修，預計年底前開出，2026 年底總店數至
少達到 19 家，若其餘送審門市進度順利，更有機會衝刺 20 家大關。

截至 2026 年 8 月底，主品牌「八
方雲集」台灣門市達 1,058 家，
較去年底淨增 25 家； 「梁社漢排
骨」達 260 家，較去年底淨增 12
家。

億元(+7.1%YoY)， 獲利5.98 億元

### Page 4

請參見末頁重要披露及免責聲明 4

受固定成本與前期投入影響，1H26 美國營運尚處小額虧損階段 
美國子公司 1H26 虧損 5,700 萬元，主要是因為營收規模尚不足以完全吸
收固定成本，相關影響包括：(1)加州有額外成本支出。管理層表示，去
年北加州門市開出後，加州區域營運已接近損平，但 1H26 有 3 家新店延
後設計及送審，造成近 1 千萬元的前期支出；(2)為取得跨州供應資格，
加州中央工廠導入 USDA 規範，初期生產排程受限，且清潔流程、人力配
置要求提高，在生產效率下降時，成本亦同步增加。管理層表示，USDA 規
範導入初期的磨合階段已大致完成，目前生產效率正逐步改善 。(3)在達
拉斯首店於 9 月開幕前，持續投入的前期人力、租金等籌備支出。

德州廠產能到位，聚焦 USDA 認證與跨州物流戰略 
德州中央工廠已完工並正式投入生產，目前產能約可支援 30 至 50 家門
市 ， 因此短期內美國沒有重大資本支出的規劃。之後德州中央工廠亦將申
請 USDA 認證資格，屆時該廠食材將能採跨州冷凍配送供應至其他門店。
沿德州至加州物流路線積極洽談新店點(如在亞利桑那、 內華達等)，進一
步加快展店速度並產生規模經濟。

圖五、八方雲集美國區季度營收變化

資料來源：法說資料

香港市場：景氣偏弱策略審慎，精簡門市汰弱留強 
香港市場受當地消費景氣偏弱影響 ， 近兩年營運策略轉向審慎 ， 展店重心
轉為汰弱留強， 使香港地區1H26 營收下滑至 5.98 億元(-11.1%YoY)，1H26
獲利亦下滑至 1,570 萬元(-16.0%YoY)。雖然仍維持獲利，但受到門市營
收下滑及高租金壓力影響 ，利潤空間受到擠壓 。此次調整主要是關閉租金
成本過高的門市，故截至 2026 年 8 月底，集團在香港各品牌門市合計下
滑至 71 家，較 2025 年底淨減少 7 家。

美國地區1H26 營收則提升至4.38
億元(+51.7%YoY)， 展現強勁的成
長動能。

圖七、八方雲集台灣、香港、美國事業年度損益/近兩年上半年損益

資料來源：法說資料

二、業績回顧：

1H26 業績依舊有偏強增速，惟 2Q26 毛利率因成本壓力而下降 
八方雲集 2026 年上半年合併營收達 45.96 億元，成長 7.3%YoY；1H26 營
業利益達 5.56 億元，成長 15.4%YoY；1H26 稅後淨利 4.67 億元，成長
20.5%YoY；1H26EPS 7.01 元 。獲利增速高於營收表現，主要是受惠台灣業
務的規模經濟顯現 、 自動化中央工廠效益提升，令1H26 毛利率升至36.0%，
年增 0.4 個百分點；費用率則降至 23.9%，年減 0.5 個百分點。

單季度來看 ， 八方雲集2Q26 合併營收23.33 億元(+3.1%QoQ，+10.7% YoY)；
營業利益 2.72 億元(-4.0%QoQ，+13.0%YoY)，稅後淨利 2.30 億元(-
2.9%QoQ，+19.4%YoY)，2Q26EPS 3.45 元，略低於 1Q26EPS 3.56 元。

香港地區 1H26 營收下滑至 5.98
億元(-11.1%YoY)，1H26 獲利亦下
滑至 1,570 萬元(-16.0%YoY)。

台灣市場為公司主要 獲利來源 ，
1H26 獲利占比逾 90%， 成長22.3%

16.0%YoY； 美國1H26 呈小額虧損

### Page 6

請參見末頁重要披露及免責聲明 6

獲利較前季下滑，主要是因為：(1)公司未調漲台灣區售價，但肉品價格
上漲，包材及運輸成本亦上升，壓縮利潤空間；(2)加州中央工廠因導入
USDA 規範而使生產效率下降。工廠需增加清潔程序 、 生產人力 ， 且生產排
程受到更多限制，亦增加部分成本，進而使2Q26 毛利率下降至 35.6%(季
減 0.8 個百分點，年減 0.4 個百分點)。

第 2 季推銷費用金額雖有增加，但因台灣區營收成長較快，2Q26 推銷費
用率下降至 17.9%(季減 0.1 個百分點，年減0.9 個百分點)。第2 季管理
費用較去年同期增加約 2 千萬元 ，主要來自德州前期籌備 、 人員進駐等支
出。管理層表示下半年仍會嚴控費用 ，預期費用不會較上半年出現明顯跳
升。

圖八、八方雲集 季度合併營收與毛利率表現

資料來源：法說資料

三、營運展望：

台灣市場：聚焦特定商圈展店，擴大 To Go 新店型深化滲透 
管理層認為台灣市場仍具進一步的展店與滲透空間 ， 未來將持續推進擴張
步伐。在選址策略上，公司將精準鎖定科技園區、辦公大樓、學區及封閉
型商圈進行開發。此外，公司將大力擴大布局「To Go 」小型外帶店。此
店型藉由縮小坪數 ， 能有效降低裝潢與人力成本 ， 不僅能大幅提升加盟主
的加盟意願 ， 更有利於切入過去因高昂租金而難以進駐的精華商圈 。 在門
市穩健增加的動能帶動下 ，研究部預期八方雲集台灣區今明兩年營收將分
別溫和增長 8.3%YoY 與 5.0%YoY。

2Q26 毛利率下降至 35.6%，主要
是因為國內肉品價格 上漲，加上
加州中央工廠額外成本支出。

### Page 7

請參見末頁重要披露及免責聲明 7

美國市場：達拉斯快餐模式初步驗證成效，2027 年展店能見度高 
美國市場展店能見度已成功延伸至 2027 年。目前德州與亞利桑那州鳳凰
城已分別完成 3 家及 2 家 、共計5 家新店的簽約 。 考量美國新店從簽約到
開幕約需 7～8 個月 ， 此5 家門市預計將於 2027 年陸續加入營運 ； 同時 ，
公司也持續在加州北部與聖地牙哥等區域尋找新據點 。 在展店效益持續發
酵下，研究部預估今明兩年美國區營收將維持在 40%左右的強勁增速。

在營運效益上，八方雲集於達拉斯首店首度導入「快餐模式」 ，以批次製
作搭配流水線取餐，每小時可服務高達 100～120 位顧客。試營運期間僅
午餐 3 小時營收便達約 5,000 美元。管理層預估全天營業後 ， 日均營收可
望衝上 1.0 萬至 1.5 萬美元 ， 初期表現顯著優於加州門市的原先假設 。 若
此新模式持續驗證成功，公司計畫進一步導入加州部分門市 。

由於德州中央工廠已於 9 月正式投產 ， 短期美國事業因新店籌備 、USDA 規
範 、 物流及德州中央工廠固定成本先行投入等因素影響， 預期仍會呈現小
額虧損。然而，隨 2027 年展店家數增加、區域門市密度及中央工廠稼動
率提升 ， 固定成本可望逐步攤薄 ，尤其新快餐模式有望降低德州工廠所需
的損益兩平店數，預期2027 年美國事業虧損幅度將能收斂，並有望2028
年實現年度損平。

香港市場：大規模閉店告終，但策略仍謹慎，展望平淡 
香港市場上半年的結構性調整主要為關閉租金成本過高的門市 。 目前這一
波大規模的閉店調整已大致告一段落 ，未來集團不會再積極縮減店數 ， 但
仍會持續檢視單店效益 ，追求單店獲利能力的改善。考量香港市場未來策
略仍偏謹慎保守，故研究部預期香港區營收今年營收衰退 10.0%YoY，明
年衰退 3.0%YoY。

四、獲利預估：

下半年營收逐季升，且成本壓力趨緩，預期本業獲利將改善 
展望 2026 年，台灣「八方雲集」與「梁社漢排骨」展店動能優於預期，
預期台灣區營收仍維持穩健成長。美國方面，9 月達拉斯首店開幕後，還
會再新增 2~3 家門市，將能帶動合併營收逐季提升。雖 2Q26 毛利率受到
肉品、包材成本上漲影響，但隨下半年原物料價格趨穩，加上公司嚴控費
用，盈利能力有望逐步改善。研究部預估 2026 年合併營收 95.20 億元
（+8.3%YoY ） ，毛利率35.9% 、費用率 23.6% ，營業利益 11.73 億元
（+8.8%YoY） ，稅後淨利9.61 億元（+12.2%YoY），EPS 14.07 元。

美國進入加速展店期，新模式效果逐步顯現 
展望 2027 年，台灣市場聚焦八方與梁社漢兩大主力品牌，將繼續拓點，
滲透更多商圈 ； 美國市場得益於門市數持續增加 ， 以及快餐模式可望成為
後續美國展店的新標準，擴大營收規模。研究部預估 2027 年合併營收
102.33 億元 （+7.5%YoY） ，毛利率回升至36.0%，費用率降至23.1%，營業
利益 13.23 億元（+12.8%YoY） ，稅後淨利10.52 億元（+9.5%YoY），EPS 
15.05 元。

五、投資建議：

考量八方雲集台灣本業下半年業績將能改善 ，4Q26 營收與獲利還會創高 ，
加上美國市場營收成長且中期有望實現獲利，研究部給予 15 倍本益比。
目標價 225 元，維持買進評等。

## Document 108: 9月外贸数据点评：出口超预期，结构改善加快

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月外贸数据点评：出口超预期，结构改善加快.pdf
Report source: 联储证券
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 1

9 月外贸数据点评：出口超预期，结构改善加快

[Table_Author] 
 魏争 分析师  沈夏宜 分析师

Email:weizheng@lczq.com Email:shenxiayi@lczq.com  
证书:S1320524100001 证书:S1320523020004

报告要点： 
出口明显超预期，增长的覆盖范围进一步扩大 。9 月，以美元计价，出口
同比增长 36.3%，较 8 月提高 11.3 个百分点，远高于万得一致预期
25.7%；环比增长 11.4%，也明显强于往年同期。出口超预期主要来自两
方面支撑，一方面，全球制造业景气继续改善，AI 产业链需求保持强劲。
9 月全球制造业 PMI 由 8 月的 52.3 升至 53.0，继续创近 4 年来新高，另
一方面，传统产业出口延续修复趋势，增长的覆盖范围进一步扩大，与 AI
产业链共同支撑出口增长。 
分产品看，机电产品仍是主要动力，传统商品修复明显加快 。一是，AI 产
业链维持高景气，优势制造业继续提供核心支撑。9 月，集成电路、自动
数据处理设备出口分别增长 136.0%和 92.9%，合计拉动整体出口增长
13.0 个百分点；汽车、家用电器出口分别增长 62.0%和 31.7%。机电产
品整体增长 43.7%，拉动出口 27.6 个百分点，贡献约四分之三的出口增
量。二是，传统商品普遍加快，出口修复的覆盖范围扩大。箱包、纺织
品、服装、鞋靴、家具和玩具出口分别增长 22.5%、19.0%、23.8%、
20.2%、22.0%和 40.8%，均高于 8 月。六类商品合计增长 23.0%，拉动
整体出口 2.6 个百分点，较 8 月增加约 1.4 个百分点。传统商品对出口的
支撑有所增强，但机电产品仍主导本轮增长。我们分析传统商品出口增速
改善有两方面原因，一方面，低基数放大了部分商品的同比修复。2025
年 9 月箱包、服装、鞋靴和玩具出口分别下降 12.3%、8.0%、13.3%和
28.0%。其中，本月玩具虽增长 40.8%，较 2024 年同期的两年复合增速
仅约 0.7%，或指向本月高增主要是对前期下滑的修复性回补。另一方
面，节日备货及交付前移可能影响月度出货。部分义乌圣诞用品商户今年
接单情况好于去年1；受生产、海运均需预留周期等因素影响，部分节日
商品提前安排交付，可能对近期玩具、箱包等消费品出口形成阶段性支
撑。 
分地区看，主要市场普遍改善，东盟和欧盟对出口加速的贡献尤为突出 。
一是，亚洲市场维持强劲增长，东盟贡献明显扩大。9 月，对东盟出口增
长 52.3%，较 8 月提高 22.1 个百分点，创 2022 年以来最高增速，其中，
对越南出口增长 53.3%；对中国香港、韩国分别增长 51.0%和 68.2%。东
盟、中国香港和韩国合计拉动整体出口 16.5 个百分点，较 8 月增加 3.9
个百分点，其中东盟拉动由 5.4 个百分点升至 8.5 个百分点。中国香港同
比增速虽回落，出口金额环比仍增长 18.1%。二是，对欧洲及其他主要市
相关报告  
9 月 FOMC：通胀回落仍不足，政策路径
整体偏鹰，AI 投资成为新的宏观变量 
2026.10.12 
美国 8 月 CPI：核心通胀超预期反弹，市
场重新定价利率路径 
2026.09.16 
8 月金融数据点评：财政与准财政加力有
望结束社融增速单边下行 
2026.09.16

### Page 2

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 2 
场出口明显改善。9 月，对欧盟出口增长 25.3%，较 8 月提高 18.6 个百
分点，拉动由 1.1 个百分点升至 3.7 个百分点；对德国、俄罗斯和加拿大
分别增长 31.4%、51.1%和 31.8%。三是，对美国出口继续修复。9 月，
对美出口增长 39.6%，较 8 月提高 5.2 个百分点，拉动整体出口 4.1 个百
分点；环比增长 12.7%，较上月明显加快。低基数仍是对美出口修复的重
要支撑。去年同期对美出口下降 27.0%，本月出口金额较 2024 年同期仅
高 1.8%，趋势性恢复仍需观察。 
从量价看，数量贡献增强、量价结构边际改善，整体增长仍保留较强的价
格驱动特征。一方面，传统消费品及部分装备产品的数量改善更为明显。
9 月，箱包和鞋靴出口数量分别增长约 11.5%和 10.1%，出口均价分别上
涨 9.9%和 9.2%，金额增长开始获得数量端支撑。家用电器、汽车和铝材
出口数量分别增长 25.4%、55.5%和 26.1%出口，均价分别上涨 5.0%、
4.2%和 16.3%，数量贡献较为突出。另一方面，电子产品仍具有较强的
均价驱动特征。集成电路出口数量仅增长约 1.1%，均价上涨 133.5%；手
机数量下降 12.6%，均价上涨 30.6%，价格仍是金额增长的主要支撑。在
跟踪的 11 类商品中，8 类呈现量价齐升，但船舶、液晶显示模组均价有
所下降，钢材数量基本持平。 
进口维持高增，机电产品仍是核心支撑，能源由拖累转为拉动。 9 月，进
口增长 31.0%，较 8 月提高 2.8 个百分点。结构上看，进口高增仍主要集
中于机电产业链，能源金额改善更多为体现价格效应。一是，机电产品拉
动继续增强，增量更多来自自动数据处理设备。9 月，机电产品进口增长
52.2%，拉动整体进口 21.6 个百分点。其中，自动数据处理设备增长
296.5%，拉动 8.9 个百分点，较 8 月增加约 2.8 个百分点；集成电路增长
74.7%，拉动 12.8 个百分点，其数量仅增长 1.7%，均价上涨 71.7%。二
是，能源价格支撑增强，带动金额表现改善。原油和天然气进口金额分别
增长 3.3%和 34.0%，均价分别上涨 31.5%和 36.3%，数量仍分别下降
21.5%和 1.7%。原油、成品油和天然气合计拉动进口约 0.8 个百分点，较
8 月拖累约 1.0 个百分点明显改善。三是，金属资源品表现分化，铜矿仍
主要由价格拉动。铜矿进口增长 27.1%，数量下降 4.2%，均价上涨
32.6%；铁矿石进口下降 7.4%，量价均有所回落。四是，农产品温和增
长，数量修复仍有限。农产品进口增长 5.5%，拉动整体进口约 0.5 个百
分点；大豆进口增长 2.8%，数量下降 7.7%，均价上涨 11.4%。 
往后看，出口仍有支撑，结构改善具备延续条件 。一方面，AI 投资及区域
制造业扩张继续支撑电子、设备和中间品需求；S&P Global 于 2026 年
10 月 9 日发布的全球贸易 PMI 分析指出，科技企业因担忧供给瓶颈和价
格上涨而增加缓冲库存，或可以为订单提供额外支撑2，但这部分需求的
持续性仍需观察。另一方面，部分传统商品及对美出口仍有低基数支撑，
贸易促进和摩擦管控有助于巩固订单。10 月 15 日开幕的广交会将为家
具、纺织、箱包、鞋靴和玩具等行业提供供采对接机会；10 月上旬中欧
磋商在汽车贸易等方面形成成果，有利于稳定相关市场预期 。往后看，一

### Page 3

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 3 
方面需关注 AI 产业链投资及相关出口需求的持续性；另一方面需关注传
统商品数量修复能否延续，以及提前交付对后续出货节奏的影响。

风险提示：全球制造业景气及外需超预期回落；全球 AI 投资需求不及预
期；贸易摩擦及出口限制超预期升级；地缘冲突超预期。

投资评级说明 
投资建议的评级标准  评级 说明 
评级标准为报告发布日后的 6 个月内公司股价 （或行业
指数） 相对同期基准指数的相对市场表现。 其中A 股市
场以沪深 300 指数为基准； 新三板市场以三板成指 （针
对协议转让标的） 或三板做市指数 （针对做市转让标的）
为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准。 
股票评级 
买入 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 10%以上 
增持 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 5%~10%之间 
中性 相对同期基准指数涨幅在-5%~+5%之间 
减持 相对同期基准指数跌幅在 5%以上 
行业评级 
看好 相对表现优于市场 
中性 相对表现与市场持平 
看淡 相对表现弱于市场

## Document 109: 9月美国核心CPI环比再超预期，或加剧10月升息担忧

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月美国核心CPI环比再超预期，或加剧10月升息担忧.pdf
Report source: 浦银国际证券
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

从环比细分数据来看，9 月核心商品和住房 CPI 通胀率的回升是核心 CPI
走高的主要推动力，排除了住房的核心服务 CPI（超级核心服务 CPI）
通胀率不升反降（图表 1）。

 核心商品价格环比通胀率反弹 0.15 个百分点到 0.25%， 部分体现
AI 影响，但油价传导仍不算明显。其中，8 月大降的教育和通讯
商品（9 月：0.87%，8 月：0.10%）、娱乐商品（9 月：0.86%，8
月：0.00%）CPI 环比通胀率在 9 月强势反弹是主要影响因素。受
强 AI 周期和内存涨价因素影响，计算机价格环比增速录得 0.9%，
是教育和通讯商品价格上升的主要推动力。服装 CPI 环比通胀率
亦由于换季有所反弹。运输 CPI 环比通胀率在二手车价格环比增
速几乎翻倍的情况下亦略微上升，但家具和医疗护理价格的环比
跌幅有所扩大。

 住房 CPI 环比通胀率 9 月上升 0.13 个百分点到 0.39%。最为关键
的业主等价租金 CPI 环比通胀率 9 月几乎翻倍到 0.35%是主要推
动力。不过价格增长主要集中于美国中西部地区，或部分归因于
统计问题。考虑到房价趋 势，我们认为该分项的上涨不 会持续。
主要居所租金 CPI 环比通胀率微升到 0.25%。

 超级核心服务 CPI 环比通胀率 9 月回落到 0.34%（8 月：0.42%）。
教育和通讯商品服务 CPI 环比通胀率的下滑（9 月：0.05%，8 月：
1.85%）是主要推动力，符合我们此前预期。其中，0.5%的学费环
比增速仍高，但无线通信费增速明显下行。运输服务 CPI 通胀率
继续走高（9 月：0.59%，8 月：0.45%），或受到油价上涨影响。
机票 CPI 环比通胀率从 8 月的 2.7%下降到 9 月的 1.2%， 但仍维持
在较高水平上。此外，医疗护理和娱乐服务 CPI 环比通胀率亦有
所上升，但整体来说还算温和。

此外， 期待已久的9 月 PCE 统计方式调整落地。调整后，7 月核心 PCE
同比通胀率下调 0.36 个百分点，到 2.98%。调整幅度比我们之前预计
浦银国际 
数据点评 
9月美国核心CPI环比再超预期，或加剧10月升息担忧 
金晓雯，PhD，CFA 
首席宏观分析师 
xiaowen_jin@spdbi.com 
(852) 2808 6437

2026 年 10 月 15 日

扫码关注浦银国际研究

### Page 2

9 月非农就业数据明显不及预期， 劳动力市场重新显现降温迹象。9 月
非农新增就业仅 2.9 万人，显著低于市场预期的 9 万人（图表 2）；与
此同时，8 月新增就业由 16.2 万人下修至 13.3 万人，7 月则下修至减
少 1 万人，前两个月合计下修 6 万人。分行业来看，就业增长整体较
为分散但幅度有限，政府、信息业和部分专业服务就业下降， 临时工
就业亦连续数月回落，显示企业招聘需求有所转弱。不过，就业市场
目前更接近缓慢降温而非加速恶化。在 9 月劳动参与率上升 0.2 个百
分点到 61.8%的情况下，9 月失业率仅由 8 月的 4.14%小幅升至 4.18%
（图表 3）。平均时薪环比仅增长 0.1%、同比增速降至 3.0%，工资压
力继续缓和。我们预计今年剩余时间就业仍将受经济动能放缓、招聘
需求减弱及 AI 应用增强等因素影响而走弱， 但在裁员率仍较低的情况
下，就业市场更可能表现为新增就业持续偏弱， 而非失业率快速上升。

9 月超预期的核心 CPI 数据再次凸显通胀粘性。虽然我们认为推高 9 月
核心 CPI 的部分因素——二手车价格和业主等价租金的上涨或不持久，
且 8-9 月推高核心 CPI 的数据分项比较分散， 或更倾向于波动因素， 而
不是单一分项的持续走强，但是连续两个月超预期的核心 CPI 通胀率
或加深美联储对通胀的担忧，尤其在是高油价对核心通胀率的滞后影
响仍未有显著体现的情况下。

通胀风险有所抬升， 重点关注高油价持续性及其与 AI 投资需求的叠加
效应。10 月以来，布伦特油价仍在每桶 100 美元以上波动，地缘局势
与石油供给恢复的不确定性仍高。高能源成本若持续，或沿运输、制
造、服务定价的路径滞后传导，延缓核心通胀回落。与此同时，AI 投
资对电力、电子元件和建设资源的需求可能加剧局部供给约束。 美联
储官员哈马克 10 月 12 日指出，通胀压力已从能源等局部领域向更广
泛的需求侧扩散，并需警惕企业提价行为变化。后续还需重点观察涨
价是否向更多分项扩散。不过，工资增速放缓、高能源支出对其他消
费的挤压或形成需求端约束，从而压制再通胀。

9 月会议纪要与最新官员讲话显示，美联储仍倾向于进一步收紧金融
条件， 但加息节奏保持灵活。美联储 10 月 7 日公布的 9 月会议纪要显
示，多数与会者认为年底前可能仍需再次加息，但后续决策取决于数
据和风险平衡。纪要认为，能源价格和 AI 投资扩张增加了价格压力，
而整体金融条件仍支持经济增长。 美联储官员沃勒 10 月 8 日表示， 加
息不必发生在连续的会议上；哈马克 10 月 12 日则倾向于在条件允许
时较快作出小幅调整，以防高通胀固化为企业和居民的预期。

### Page 3

若 10 月未提前行动， 我们预计美联储会在12 月会议加息 25 个基点。
若 10 月暂停， 而后续核心CPI 和核心 PCE 仍显示粘性、 就业没有明显
恶化，12 月加息 25 个基点，仍是我们的基准情景。若10 月已经加息，
12 月的情况则需重新评估；只有价格压力进一步扩散、通胀预期上移
且需求仍有韧性，才支持两次会议连续加息。

风险提示：下行风险：国际油价和地缘政治局势超预期变化；通胀粘性
超预期触发美联储连续加息周期；就业或消费骤然恶化；AI 投资和生
产率的净影响偏离预期；美债价格继续上升引发金融危机。上行风险：
AI 资本开支和生产率提升对美国经济的支撑超预期。

他财务方面的任何决策或行动。 除关于历史数据的陈述外， 本报告可能包含前瞻性的陈述， 牵涉多种风险和不确定性，
该等前瞻性陈述可基于一些假设，受限于重大风险和不确定性。

本报告之观点、推荐、建议和意见均不一定反映浦银国 际证券的立场。浦银国际控股有限公司及其联属公司、关联公

## Document 121: [000270] 기아(000270) 잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기기아(000270) 잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000270] 기아(000270) 잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기기아(000270) 잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung

### Page 1

기아 (000270)

EARNINGS PREVIEW Company Analysis | 자동차/로봇 | 2026.10.15  
잘 싸웠지만 아쉬운 3분기

3Q26 Preview 
기아는 3Q26E 매출액 31.1조원(+8.2% YoY, -6.0% QoQ), 영업이익 2.2조원
(+53.1% YoY, -14.9% QoQ, OPM 7.2%)을 기록, 컨센서스를 하회할 전망이다. 
물량은 좋았으나, 아쉬운 믹스와 환율 
글로벌 도매 판매 84.7만대(+7.8% 이하 YoY), 중국 제외 판매량은 83.0만대
(+8.6%)를 기록했다. EV·HEV 확대에 따른 ASP 상승은 지속되고 있다. 전쟁으로 인
한 원자재 상승으로 3분기 재료비 부담은 전분기 대비 3,000억~4,000억원 증가할 
것으로 예상된다. EV 판매 비중은 2분기 13%에서 3분기 15%, 유럽은 35%에서 
43%로 확대되며 유럽 내 마진 희석은 불가피하다. 원화 강세에 따른 영업상 부담은 
판매보증충당부채 환입 등으로 상쇄돼 3분기 환율 영향은 제한적일 전망이다. 판매보
증충당부채 환입은 QoQ +5,000억원 수준으로 추정된다. 4분기부터 환율 효과는 부정
적일 것이며, 환율 10원 변동 시 연간 손익 민감도는 약 1,500억원 수준이다. 
투자의견은 Buy를 유지하고 목표주가는 165,000원으로 하향 
미국 조지아 공장의 텔루라이드 HEV(2.8만대)와 HMGMA의 스포티지 HEV(1.0만대) 
신규 생산으로 북미의 견조한 HEV 수요에 대응하며, 2027년 믹스 개선이 기대된다. 
유럽은 EV3·EV5로 점유율을 확대하는 가운데 유럽 내 할인율 하락과 EV 잔존가치 
회복으로 판매 여건이 개선되고 있다. 향후 IAA 규제에 따른 법인·플릿 혜택 제한은 
중국 업체의 원가 우위를 줄일 수 있을 전망이다. 
원화 약세 지속을 반영해 이익 추정치를 하향하며, 이에 따라 목표주가를 하향한다.  
다만, 현재 주가는 12M Fwd P/E 기준 4.8배로 Legacy OEM 평균(7.7배)보다 낮은 
수준, 저가 매수 기회라 판단된다. 현재 배당 수익률은 6% 수준으로, 상대적으로 높은 
배당 매력은 주가 하방을 지지할 전망이다. 
 Analyst 이병근 
bglee@ls-sec.co.kr 
Buy (유지)

## Document 137: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 드디어 엔진 증설, 저평가된 주가HD현대중공업(329180) 드디어 엔진 증설, 저평가된 주가드디어 엔진 증설, 저평_dc1151a4

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 드디어 엔진 증설, 저평가된 주가HD현대중공업(329180) 드디어 엔진 증설, 저평가된 주가드디어 엔진 증설, 저평_dc1151a4.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-09-11
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

2026.09.11  
HD 현대중공업 (329180)

드디어 엔진 증설, 저평가된 주가  Flash Note 
[조선/기계/방산] 변용진 2122-9181 yongjin.byun@imfnsec.com

Check Point  Buy (Maintain) 
HD현대중공업 중형엔진 및 SMR 공장 증설 결정  
엔진도 끌어올리지 못하는 조선업 주가, 그래도 펀더멘털은 굳건

목표주가(12M) 860,000 원(유지) 
종가(2026.09.10) 453,500 원 
상승여력 89.6 %

HD현대중공업, 중형엔진 및 SMR 시설투자 공시  
기다리던 엔진 증설의 세부 사항이 드디어 공개되었다. HD 현대중공업은 10일 공시를 통해 총 1조 722억원(중형엔진 
8,336억원/SMR 2,386억원) 규모의 시설투자 계획을 밝혔다. 먼저 중형엔진 관련, 글로벌 전력 수요 증가에 따른 엔진발전 시장 
확대에 대응 목적으로 생산능력 확충을 위한 토지 취득 및 공장 신설 및 생산설비 구축에 28년 5월까지 투자가 진행될 예정이다. 
중형엔진의 생산 능력은 현재 연간 3GW급에서 증설이 완료되면 7.2GW급으로 확장되며, 첫 엔진 출하는 28년 하반기로 예상된다. 
증설되는 신공장의 가동률은 28년 30%로 출발하여 30년 100%로 올라설 전망이다. 이에 따라 연간 1GW당 약 1조원의 매출과 
20%이상의 영업이익(현 엔진사업부 이상) 수준으로 추정되며, 계획대로 증설이 완료되고 일감을 확보한다면 총 4조원 이상의 매출 
증대 효과가 기대된다. SMR 투자 관련해서는 SMR 주기기 제작사업 진입 및 수주물량 증가에 대응하기 위해 SMR 전용 공장, 설비 
구축 및 기존 공장 최적화에 29년 4월까지 2,386억원이 투입된다. 이는 연간으로 Terrapower의 Natrium SMR 주기기 2기(각 
345MW)를 제작할 수 있는 규모다. HD현대중공업은 이번 증설이 데이터센터용 엔진만을 위한 것이 아님을 강조했다. 데이터센터 뿐 
아니라 국가전력망, 원전용 비상발전기 등 전세계적으로 증가하는 육상용 발전엔진 수요에 대응하기 위함이며, 최근 미국의 육상 
데이터센터 사업이 님비현상 등으로 일부 좌초되고 있음에도 그보다 더 큰 그림에 베팅한 것이다.  
엔진도 끌어올리지 못하는 조선업 주가, 그래도 펀더멘털은 굳건  
분명한 기업가치 증분의 근거가 되는 증설공시였음에도 불구하고 HD현대중공업의 주가는 발표 이후 하락으로 마감하였다. 올해 
조선업 주가는 YTD 평균 10%이상 하락하며 약세를 보이고 있는 상태다. (HD현대중공업 -10.0%, 삼성중공업 -11.6%, 한화오션 -
25.5%)  
조선사 공히 2분기의 실적 서프라이즈와 함께 올해 수주목표를 사실상 초과 달성하는 등 우수한 펀더멘털을 유지하고 있으나 이는 
주가 상승재료로 반영되고 있지 않다. 7~8월 반도체발 주식시장 혼란에 따른 전체적 수급 약세도 무시할 수 없는 요인이다. 여기에 
지난해 주가 상승의 주요 동력으로 작용하였던 미국 함정 사업 관련 협력 진전이 느린 점 등이 주가 약세의 주요 원인으로 
분석된다. 백악관 및 美해군에서는 지속적으로 함정 해외 건조의 필요성을 역설하고 있으나 美입법부에서는 지역구 의원의 반대 
등으로 관련 법안의 입법 동력이 약해져 있는 상황이다. 美국회의 이번 회기(119대 회기, ~2027년 1월 3일까지)가 이미 막바지에 
다다르고 있으며 11월 중간선거 일정까지 감안하면 실질적으로 이번 회기중에 관련 법안이 통과 및 발효될 가능성은 미미하기 
때문에, 2026년 남은 하반기에 함정사업 관련 조선업 주가 동력을 기대하기는 힘들어졌다. 다가오고 있는 3분기 실적도 원화 강세 
및 여름휴가/사고 등에 따른 조업일수 하락, 후판 유통가 상승 등 감익 요인이 산적해 있어 2분기에 비해 쉽지 않은 상황이다.  
그러나 여전히 본업인 조선업의 체력은 훌륭하다. 전세계 선박 발주량은 8월 누적기준 5,972만 CGT로 전년동기 대비 +60.6% 많은 
발주가 나오고 있으며 이는 2021년 이후 가장 강한 발주세다. 한국 조선사의 주력 선종인 LNG, 탱커, 컨테이너선의 발주 비중이 
전체 발주량의 67%를 차지하고 있으며 이에 힘입어 조선사들은 수주목표를 사실상 초과 달성하였다. (상선기준, HD현대중공업 달성 
130.6억 달러/목표 114.7억, 삼성중공업 달성 61억/목표 57억, 한화오션 달성 60억/목표없음) 3분기 실적은 다소 쉬어갈 수 
있으나 여전히 수주잔고에 기반한 추정상 매출/영업이익은 2028년까지 꾸준한 상승이 예상된다. 작년보다 오히려 하락한 주가가 
현저한 저평가일 수밖에 없는 이유다. 조선업 비중확대 및 HD현대중공업 Top Pick 의견을 유지한다.

## Document 161: AI融资隐忧重压芯片股，全球科技板块本周已蒸发逾万亿美元

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI融资隐忧重压芯片股，全球科技板块本周已蒸发逾万亿美元.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

美联储加息预期升温、 AI 融资成本攀升等多重压⼒叠加，本周全球科技股遭遇⼤规模抛售
—— 全球前 20 ⼤ AI 及半导体股市值已经合计蒸发逾 1 万亿美元，半导体 ETF （ SOXX ）创 11
年最⻓连跌纪录。周四， SK 海⼒⼠单⽇继续暴跌逾 10% ，英伟达、三星、软银⽆⼀幸免。
摘要：
加息预期、 AI 融资成本攀升与竞争格局恶化，多重压⼒在本周同步发酵，将全球科技股推⼊⼀场
⼤规模抛售。
周四全球科技股的抛售仍在继续，美股⽅⾯，英伟达当⽇收跌 1.84% ， AMD 跌 1.5% ，英特尔跌
约 2.8% ，戴尔科技重挫 6.8% 。亚洲市场跌幅更为惨烈， SK 海⼒⼠单⽇暴跌逾 10% ，三星电⼦下
跌 8.73% ，⽇本软银集团⼤跌 7.9% 。
据 CNBC 统计，截⾄本周四，全球市值最⾼的 20 只 AI 及半导体股票合计蒸发逾 1 万亿美元。追踪
芯⽚板块的 iShares 半导体 ETF （ SOXX ）周四录得连续第⼋个交易⽇下跌，区间累计跌幅约
12.7% ，据道琼斯市场数据，这是该 ETF 11 年来最⻓的连跌纪录。

英伟达 CEO ⻩仁勋在斯德哥尔摩出席活动时向彭博表⽰，他认为英伟达⽆需为 CoreWeave 提供
背书，并称 " 他们的资本成本实际上正在下降 " 。这番表态带动新云计算股（ neocloud ）盘中反
弹， CoreWeave 收涨 5.94% ， Nebius 上涨 1.29% 。
多重利空叠加，抛售逻辑层层递进
本轮抛售并⾮单⼀事件触发，⽽是多条负⾯线索在同⼀时间窗⼝内相互强化。
通胀数据是导⽕索。 周三公布的美国 9 ⽉消费者价格指数（ CPI ）⾼于预期，市场随即加⼤对美
联储进⼀步加息的押注。纳斯达克综合指数周三收跌 1.88% ⾄ 26692.87 点，标普 500 指数跌
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

1.11% ⾄ 7718.94 点。
Anthropic 数据令市场情绪雪上加霜。 据英国《⾦融时报》报道， AI 初创公司 Anthropic 在周三
⼀场私下投资者会议上披露，第三季度营收达 168 亿美元，环⽐增⻓约 50%—— 但这⼀增速远低
于第⼆季度约 145% 的环⽐增幅。 D.A. Davidson 分析师 Gil Luria 在客⼾报告中写道： " 市场似乎
将 Anthropic 的更新解读为负⾯信号，考虑到市场本就对 AI 交易⾼度敏感，这种反应可以理解。 "
Luria 本⼈对此持更为中性的看法。他指出， Anthropic 第三季度实现正向现⾦流，这表明该公司
和 OpenAI" 并⾮天⽣⽆法盈利的商业模式 " 。他还写道，从更宏观的 AI ⾏业来看， " 整体 AI 使⽤量
（以及 AI ⽀出）未必出现减速 " 。
中国竞争同步发酵。  ⻓鑫存储上周末宣布，在新架构上取得突破，可在更⼩空间内存储更多数
据。埃隆 · ⻢斯克随即在 X 平台发⽂称，中国将在 2028 年⽣产 " ⼤量内存芯⽚ " 。

与此同时，博通和英特尔的⾸席执⾏官本周⼀均警告称，融资问题可能成为 AI 扩张的重⼤阻
⼒，博通 CEO Hock Tan 表⽰，这⼀挑战甚⾄可能超过芯⽚或电⼒短缺。
利率上⾏，直接冲击 AI 基础设施投资回报
⾼利率环境对 AI 基础设施建设的冲击，是本轮抛售的核⼼逻辑之⼀。
投⾏ Post Oak Group 执⾏董事 James Vrachas 向 MarketWatch 表⽰，芯⽚股已经 " 消化了⼤量未
来增⻓预期 " ，股价本⾝已反映这些预期。在此背景下， " 情绪的轻微转变或对⽀出的质疑，都会
对股价产⽣显著冲击 " 。他说： " 相信 AI 的⻓期潜⼒，和相信每⼀家受益于这⼀增⻓的公司都被合
理定价，是两回事。 "
Baird 投资策略师 Ross Mayfield 向 MarketWatch 指出，国债收益率上⾏对芯⽚板块构成压⼒，尤
其是⾼成⻓股以及 "AI ⻛险谱系中更偏激进⼀端 " 、更依赖外部融资的公司。他表⽰， CPI 数据可
能加⼤美联储加息的紧迫性， " 这对短端利率造成巨⼤压⼒，对那些财务基础较弱、或持有与短

期利率挂钩的浮动利率债务的借款⼈打击尤为直接 " 。
Mayfield 还指出，即便是超⼤规模云服务商（ hyperscaler ）也并⾮对利率完全免疫。 " 即使是最
⼤、现⾦最充裕的公司，也必须重新评估这些项⽬的投资回报率， " 他说， " ⽽市场的⼤部分动能
都建⽴在超⼤规模云服务商对硬件和数据中⼼基础设施的⽀出之上，这就给整条供应链蒙上了
⼀层阴影。 "
亚欧市场跌幅更深，韩⽇股市⾸当其冲
美股的抛售情绪迅速蔓延⾄亚洲和欧洲市场，且跌幅普遍超过美股。
周四，韩国 SK 海⼒⼠单⽇暴跌逾 10% ，三星电⼦跌 8.73% ， LG Innotek 跌 8.95% ，⾸尔半导体
跌 9.28% ，三星 SDI 跌 6.26% 。韩国综合股价指数（ KOSPI ）逼近 4 ⽉以来最低收盘⽔平。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
⽇本市场同样重挫。软银集团跌 7.9% ，半导体测试设备商爱德万测试（ Advantest ）暴跌近
14% ，芯⽚制造设备供应商东京电⼦跌 6.45% ，瑞萨电⼦跌 8% 。台积电跌 1.81% 。
欧洲⽅⾯，荷兰半导体设备巨头 ASML ⼩幅下跌，英⻜凌跌 5% ， ASM International 跌 7% 。
分析师：需求信号才是真正的警报线
尽管市场情绪低迷，部分分析师认为本轮抛售尚未触及 AI 需求的实质性恶化。
Yorkville Ives 科技分析师 Dan Ives 在报告中写道，本轮抛售反映的是市场对融资成本的担忧，⽽
⾮对 AI 算⼒需求的质疑。 " 美国国债收益率升破 5% ，确实推⾼了这些基础设施项⽬的融资成本，
投资者关注资产负债表和合同结构是合理的，但利率是融资问题，不是需求问题。 "

Ives 认为当前是买⼊机会，并表⽰ AI 基础设施热潮仍处于早期阶段。他明确列出了真正值得警惕
的信号： " 真正会改变我们判断的，是需求信号，⽽不是利率信号 "—— 包括超⼤规模云服务商削
减资本⽀出计划、合同取消或 GPU 租赁价格下跌。
Gabelli Funds 投资组合经理 Hendi Susanto 则向 MarketWatch 表⽰，许多芯⽚及半导体设备公
司 " 资产负债表依然稳健，⾃由现⾦流充裕 " ，他预计其中⼤多数公司 " 将以强劲态势收官今年 " ，
尤其是那些需求仍超过供给的公司。他还指出，即将到来的科技公司财报季将揭⽰ " 投资者在多
⼤程度上将通胀担忧纳⼊定价 " 。

Wired for growth
Initiate coverage with an Outperform rating and target price of NT$270
ACES is entering a two-engine growth phase. It is a key power cable supplier to
Bloom Energy as Bloom doubles its fuel-cell capacity, and it is expanding its high-
speed connector content from AI server daughterboards to ASIC motherboards, as

a high-speed cable leg comes on stream. We forecast a 72% EPS Cagr over 2026-
28, with OPM expanding from 7.4% in 2026 to 11.0% in 2027. Our TP of NT$270
is based on 20x 2H27-1H28SP EPS, and we initiate coverage at Outperform.
Bloom Energy the key earnings driver in 2027
ACES's multiyear partnership with Bloom locks in its share as Bloom plans to lift
capacity from 2GW in 2026 to 4GW/6GW in 2027/28, based on our checks. ACES
has shipped fuel-cell power cables to Bloom since 1Q26 and will add external DLO

high-voltage cables, with ASP of US$80-100k per set. We expect Bloom power
cables to be NT$5.8bn of sales and account for 28% of group revenue in 2027.
Power products need little incremental R&D and benefit from strong operating
leverage. We expect overall OPM to expand from 7.4% in 2026 to 11.0% in 2027.
High-speed products a new driver
Previously, ACES supplied only daughterboard connectors, at 10-20 units per rack.
On ASIC platforms, where ODMs have more say in supplier selection, ACES has

secured the opportunity to ship both motherboard and daughterboard connectors
(about 30 units per rack) and broadens its connector product offerings. With Gen6
connectors carrying around a 15% ASP premium over Gen5, and ACES as one of
only four vendors qualified by CSPs at Gen6, we forecast server revenue growth of
107%/74% YoY in 2026/27. The newly added Gen6 PCIe cable product, qualified
by two CSPs, began shipping in April 2026 at 20% of GM, with room to rise towards

30% as yield improves. Hence, we expect GM to recover to 26% in 2027.
Capacity in place ahead of demand
ACES has committed firm capacity ahead of demand. For power cables, it has leased
a new facility next to its Philippine plant, with new capacity planned to come online
in 4Q26. For server products, it plans to raise connector capacity from 4m to 5m
units/mo and will expand high-speed cable production lines from two to eight.
Initiate coverage with an Outperform rating

We forecast 2027/28SP EPS of NT$11.19/NT$14.93, with our 2027 forecast 48%
ahead of consensus. Our TP of NT$270 is based on 20x 2H27-1H28SP EPS, backed
by a 72% EPS Cagr in 2026-28SP driven by power cable ramp-up and high-speed
connector shipment growth. We initiate coverage with an Outperform rating.

Bloom Energy the key earnings driver in 2027
We see tremendous advantages for ACES from Bloom Energy’s strong demand backed
by its aggressive expansion plan. ACES supplies inner power cables used inside each
fuel cell in 2026 and will begin supplying external diesel locomotive (DLO) cables that
connect fuel cell stacks into a fuel cell module (energy server). Each set (including inner
cable and external cable) carries dollar content of US$80-100k.

Furthermore, ACES has worked with Bloom on high-voltage power cables for SOFC
for around six years, even though the real contribution did not transpire until 2026. On
the back of this long-standing relationship, ACES is the key supplier during the
development phase and holds at least a 30% share in the mass production stage.
Together with these considerations, we expect inner cables to contribute revenue of
NT$5,113m/NT$8,074m in 2027/28, while external cables will add revenue of

NT$707m/NT$1,154m.
Figure 1
Power cable revenue from Bloom Energy and YoY change
Source: SinoPac
As power scarcity remains the key bottleneck for AI datacentre construction, Bloom
serves as a suitable solution and offers fuel cell systems that generate electricity from
natural gas. Its modular, on-site, 24/7 power platform stands apart in the power sector.
Against a conventional grid built on centralised generation, Bloom delivers compelling
time-to-power, cleaner air and water emissions profile, native-DC compatibility with

emerging 800V AI datacentre architectures, and a solution that communities and
nearby residents accept more readily.

As of year-end 2025, Bloom's product backlog grew about 140% YoY to roughly
US$6bn. Amid the surging demand, Bloom is improving backlog conversion and
stepping up capacity expansion to capture the strong growth opportunity. In 2Q26,
Bloom raised its full-year revenue guidance to US$3.9-4.2bn, up from US$3.4-3.8bn,
and plans to double annual capacity from about 1GW in 2025, split across its California

and Delaware sites, to 2GW by the end of 2026; and will further expand the capacity
to 4GW/6GW in 2027/28, based on our channel checks.

infrastructure, with Bloom providing its fuel cell-based power generation systems and
Brookfield providing capital and infrastructure development capabilities. The strategic
financing framework expanded on June 30, 2026, from the original $5bn (October
2025) to $25bn, a fivefold increase. On customer wins, Nebius dropped its previously
planned gas and combustion turbines in May 2026 and switched to Bloom’s SOFC

solution, signing a 10-year master agreement worth up to $2.6bn. The first phase
covers 328MW of installed capacity, or roughly 250MW of guaranteed capacity, and
is expected to come online within 2026. Bloom also expanded its Oracle contract from
an initial 1.2GW to up to 2.8GW and signed deals including AEP (up to 1 GW) and
Equinix (over 100 MW across 19 datacentres), broadening its customer base materially.
Wide range of power cable products
ACES supplies Bloom with a variety of power cables across different specifications,

including Power Conversion System (PCS) cables, hot box cables and Diesel
Locomotive (DLO) high-voltage cables, rather than a single product type. In 2026,
ACES only supplies PCS cables and hot box cables, while the company will expand its
product offerings to DLO high-voltage cables from 2027, which we expect to
contribute another NT$707m of sales for the whole year.
The PCS cables and hot box cables are the inner power cables. Hot box cables
interconnect individual fuel cells within the hot box, where power generation creates

significant heat and therefore requires materials with higher durability and heat
resistance. Kaori and MTAR supply hot boxes for fuel cell power generation systems,
which require ACES’s hot box cables as one of their key components.
On the other hand, PCS cables are installed inside the Power Conversion System (PCS)
and are supplied to AcBel, a PCS solution provider. The PCS converts DC output into
usable power and manages load changes. As SOFCs generate DC power with voltage

varying according to load, the output cannot be directly connected to a datacentre.
The external power cable portfolio is mainly DLO high-voltage cables, which transmit
high-voltage DC power between fuel cell platforms, power modules and microgrid
systems. As external cables need to withstand higher voltage levels, their dollar content
is expected to be slightly higher than that of inner power cables.
Lastly, ACES also ships cables directly to Bloom Energy for development purposes.

DLO high-voltage cable opportunity heading into 800V DC era
We also see DLO cables, which are currently used outside the fuel cell, as having
significant potential after introducing 800V architecture. Under conventional AC
architecture, grid power, onsite turbines and reciprocating engines supply AC

power, and the downstream transformer, switchgear and UPS cables belong to the
utility/EPC supply chain, which ACES does not address. Bloom can deliver native
DC power directly to the rack, so the DC output cables might become part of
Bloom’s system. We see this opening as a new content opportunity for ACES as a
qualified high-voltage power cable supplier.
Within Bloom’s energy server under 800V architecture, the longer series stack of
fuel cells and the replacement of the inverter with DC-DC conversion require a

redesign of the internal harness to high-voltage specifications, which we believe
positions ACES to ship a growing volume of high-voltage DLO cables over time.

High-speed products a new driver
In 3Q24, ACES passed product qualification with ODMs for the high-speed
connectors used on Nvidia GB200 networking cards (NICs) and accelerator cards,
marking its move from traditional PC peripherals into the server space and Nvidia
supply chain. In 2025, server revenue accounted for 10% of ACES’s total revenue.
Looking ahead, we expect ACES’s server business to grow 107% in 2026, thanks to

its new product offerings, higher content per rack of high-speed products, and
strong contribution from two major ASIC customers. Looking ahead, with the new
connector capacity coming online and increasing ASP driven by connector spec
upgrade, we expect server revenue to further grow 74%/33% in 2027/28SP.

Broadening connector footprint in ASICs
Since Nvidia has strict control over its supply chain, ACES previously supplied only
the daughterboard high-speed connectors for GB200/300 servers, at roughly 10 to
20 units per rack. On ASIC platforms, since ODMs have greater influence over
supplier selection, ACES secures share on both the motherboard and the
daughterboard, lifting content to around 30 units per rack.

Besides, ACES historically supplied only MCIO connectors to these ASIC customers,
but as further qualifications clear, the company will broaden its connector product
offerings in 2026 to include Multi-Track, board-to-board, and E1/E3 connectors.
We expect the contribution from ASIC customers to scale further in 2H26, driving
server revenue to grow 64% HoH.
Figure 13 Figure 14
ACES high-speed connector content per rack increase ACES provides MCIO and a wide range of high-speed connectors

Source: SinoPac Source: ACES
Riding the Gen6 ASP curve with a compatibility advantage
Aside from the spectacular growth in the connector shipments, we expect ACES to
benefit from the ongoing Gen6 connector upgrade cycle, with ASPs expected to
increase by 15%. The AI server trays currently adopt a mix of Gen5 and Gen6
connectors, but we expect the penetration of Gen6 connectors to gradually
increase going forward as the migration from Gen5 to Gen6 is the first major

generational shift since Genoa introduced Gen5 in 2022.

Figure 15
Gen6 connector ASP is 15% higher than Gen5
Source: SinoPac
Only three to four connector vendors are qualified by CSPs at Gen6, and ACES is
one of them. Furthermore, ACES can supply both Gen5 and Gen6 platforms under
its existing qualification, with no need to re-sample or re-qualify for Gen5. This is
because Gen6 connectors keep footprint backward compatibility with Gen5. By
contrast, other peers must re-sample and run through the full qualification process

again to move up to Gen6.
About the PCIe migration and Gen6 spec upgrade
PCIe has long been used for on-board and in-chassis interconnects. In the Vera
Rubin platform, PCIe Gen6 serves as the in-tray bridge to front-of-tray modules
such as CX-9 SuperNICs, BlueField-4 DPUs and NVMe storage. GPU-to-GPU scale-
up runs on NVLink, with UALink as the open alternative; these fabrics work
alongside PCIe.
In order to address ever-larger training models, the PCIe must scale with the fast-

growing size of model parameters and datasets. The move from Gen5 to Gen6 is
not a simple speed doubling, even though per-lane bandwidth rises from 32 GT/s
to 64 GT/s. It reworks the link from the coding scheme up. Gen1 through Gen5
doubled I/O speed using NRZ signaling. Gen6 switches to PAM4 four-level
signaling and, for the first time, adds FEC (Forward Error Correction), which lets the
receiver detect and correct transmission errors on its own.

Each generation doubles bandwidth while tightening signal-integrity specs, which
demands higher-precision connector manufacturing. This creates a real barrier to
entry for high-speed connector suppliers.

High-speed cables emerging as a potential driver
ACES previously supplied connectors only. After several years of effort, its Gen6 PCIe
cable has now been qualified by two CSP clients, and the company began shipping the
in-rack cable product in April 2026. We expect high-speed cable shipments to enlarge
after the new capacity comes online and more CSP orders in 2H26. Hence, we expect
high-speed cable revenue to grow from NT$268m in 2026 to NT$793/NT$982m in

2027/28.
Currently, its high-speed cable product carries gross margin of merely 20%, but it is
likely to edge up to around 30% as the scale enlarges, and yield improves.

Capacity in place ahead of demand
ACES is rapidly expanding its high-speed cable and connector capacity, with high-
speed cable production lines expected to increase from two in 2025 to eight in
2H26, while it will proactively ramp up connector monthly capacity from 4m units
in July 2026 to 5m units by the end of 2026 to address customer demand ahead of
order placement.
Supported by strong customer demand and sufficient capacity, ACES expects total

connector shipments to reach 25m units in 2H26 and 50m units in 2027,
representing a significant increase from 15m units in 1H26.
As for power cables for Bloom Energy, ACES has leased a new facility next to its
Philippine plant, with the new power cable capacity planned to come online in
4Q26.

Diversified growth engines
ACES’s networking product portfolio includes set-top boxes, WiFi devices, routers,
and other networking equipment. We expect the business to benefit from
technology upgrades and equipment replacement driven by advances in wireless
communications, IoT, cloud computing, and cybersecurity. However, tariff and
inflationary pressures, together with component shortages among Asian customers,

are likely to limit growth. We expect networking product revenue to grow by 34%
and 14% in 2026 and 2027, respectively.
Figure 25 Figure 26
ACES Networking product offerings Networking sales and YoY
Source: ACES Source: SinoPac
Automotive: rising electronic content
ACES’s product range spans ADAS, reversing radar and camera, body control, smart
cockpit, smart antenna, and high-voltage connectors and harnesses, extending into
applications such as EV charging guns. It has entered a US EV major as well as

European and Chinese Tier-1 supply chains. Going forward, it will continue to focus
on high-voltage, high-frequency, and high-speed connection technologies. The
company expects the automotive sector to stay flat in 2027 to reflect muted
demand and limited internal resource allocation, as it focuses on AI-related business
at this stage. In the long-term, growth from the automotive sector will be driven by
ADAS upgrades, which lifts demand for sensors and compute, alongside rising EV

penetration, which adds high-value electronic systems and power semiconductor
content.

Industrial: a steady UPS-driven base
ACES supplies industrial power cords, used mainly in uninterruptible power systems
(UPS). A UPS is an electrical device that acts as a reliable buffer when the main
power supply behaves abnormally, such as during a fault, outage, or unstable
voltage. It keeps automation controllers, sensors, and actuators continuously

powered so equipment keeps running. When a power problem occurs, the UPS
supplies stable backup power to prevent equipment failure, data corruption, or
system crashes.  We expect ACES’s industrial business to grow steadily, with growth
of 9% and 14% in 2026 and 2027, respectively.

Source: ACES Source: SinoPac

cautiously optimistic about 2027 with the launch of Windows 12, combined with
easing component shortages and broader AI adoption, which should drive consumer
replacement demand, lifting global NB shipments -5%/5% year over year in
2027/28, which is a gradual recovery following a weaker 2026.

Diversifying into new growth opportunities
We found that ACES is diversifying its products’ end-application by developing new
specification and engaging with new clients. Hence, we believe in its long-term
balanced growth.
Power rack: leveraging its long-standing relationship with Schneider

ACES has a long-standing relationship with Schneider Electric at its Philippine plant.
The partnership dates back more than 20 years, with ACES’s Philippine plant
initially serving Schneider almost exclusively. Over the years, the product portfolio
has expanded from conventional power cables to uninterruptible power systems
(UPS) and, more recently, power racks for AI datacentres.
ACES began shipping high-voltage power cables for power racks in 1Q26 and

entered the volume ramp-up phase in 2Q26. We remain positive on power rack
adoption as the PSU in every IT tray hits a physical limit as rack power reaches
1MW, occupies rack space and caps compute density. Moving power shelves and
BBU/CBU into a dedicated power rack decouples power from computing and frees

Initiate with an Outperform rating
We initiate coverage of ACES with an Outperform rating given its dual growth
engine from Bloom’s strong expansion and high-speed connector customer wins
and generation upgrades. We forecast 2027/28 EPS of NT$11.19/NT$14.93, with
our 2027 forecast 48% ahead of consensus. Our TP of NT$270 is based on 20x
2H27-1H28SP PE, backed by a 72% EPS Cagr in 2026-28SP driven by Bloom’s

significant ramp and larger content in server products. Initiate coverage with an
Outperform rating.
Company profile
ACES was founded in 1996, and it mainly produces connectors, micro-coaxial cable,
high-speed transmission cables, and RF connectors. Connectors and cable
assemblies link two separate subsystems or operating units and carry the signals
between them, allowing the overall system to function properly. They are used
across a wide range of electronic products, with main applications spanning

notebooks and PC peripherals, consumer electronics, automotive electronics, cloud
servers, networking equipment, and industrial equipment.
To keep pace with the push towards lighter, thinner, and smaller notebooks and
consumer devices, ACES developed high-temperature-resistant, fine-pitch, and
low-profile products at an early stage. At the same time, it is moving actively into
the high-frequency, high-speed connectors, high-speed cables, and power supplies

required for cloud servers, as well as the connector and cable assemblies used in
automotive electronics.

Company development
ACES has used M&A well to gain new capabilities, plants and technology, which in
turn opened doors to new customers and markets. In 2015, it bought MEC IMEX
to add cable assembly capability and a plant in the Philippines, which took it into
the industrial market. In 2017, the CPC deal gave it access to the European auto
connector market and a plant in Vietnam. In 2019, it bought Kuang Ying to add

metal stamping capacity and reach Taiwan telecom customers. The key deal came
in 2021, when ACES bought 100% of Genesis, a US high-speed interconnect maker
based in Atlanta. The main goal was to gain Genesis’s long-standing ties with US
CSP and cloud customers. In 2025, ACES opened its Phase Two R&D headquarters
in Taoyuan, which focuses on cloud connectors and high-speed cables. We believe
10 years of M&A have turned ACES from a consumer connector maker into a

supplier of high-speed interconnect solutions for AI servers and the cloud.

Cashflow and balance sheet
ACES’s balance sheet shows healthy debt exposure with an improving net cash
level. Given its plan to massively expand capacity at its Taiwan plants, we expect
Capex to remain at a high level of NT$1,075m in 2026 and then slightly decline to
NT$855m in 2027. We forecast its depreciation-to-sales ratio to be around 3% in

2027, which is at the low end of its historical level.
We expect ACES’s ROE to expand from 10% in 2026 to 21% in 2027 and 23% in
2028, as the volume ramp of AI high-speed connectors and Bloom Energy power
cables drives a richer product mix and gross margin expansion, delivering 121% EPS
growth in 2027.
We expect ACES’s FCF to turn positive in 2026 as operating cashflow scales with
the AI high-speed connector and Bloom power cable ramp while Capex normalises

after the 2026 capacity buildout. Looking ahead, we expect ACES’s free cashflow
per share to be NT$0.8-1.5 in coming years.

Competitive positioning

In high-speed connectors, ACES competes primarily with overseas peers including
Amphenol (APH), TE, and Molex. APH’s edge lies in a development-technology lead of
one to two generations, while ACES’s relative strength rests on customers’ recognition
on delivering quality comparable to the overseas majors, alongside more flexible lead
times, strong capacity-expansion support, and fast ramp-up.
The structural opportunity comes from the supply chain integration trend. As

transmission speeds rise, CSPs increasingly prefer suppliers which can integrate high-
speed connectors with high-speed cables and ship assembled, finished goods.

ACES Enters from Gen6

### Page 29

Wired for growth ACES - O-PF
6 October 2026 29
ACES is one of the few Taiwanese players with this vertical-integration footprint: it
holds wire-drawing technology transferred in-house from ITRI, produces everything
from wire drawing through assembly on its own, and its commitment to moving into
high-speed cables has been noted by AWS and Meta, backed by roughly two years of
qualification work already completed.
By contrast, other Taiwanese peers have yet to build comparable vertical integration.

Customers have historically split connectors and cables across separate suppliers, with
cable suppliers represented by BizLink (3665 TT) and Longwell (6290 TT), while peers
such as Lotes (3533 TT), Alltop (3526 TT), Argosy (3217 TT) and JPC Connectivity
(6197 TT) are likewise strong in connectors but lack in-house cable capability.
Since the Gen5 qualified-supplier base was already fragmented, the Gen6 bar of
combined connector-plus-cable capability becomes ACES’s entry point, bringing ACES

into design-in from Gen6. The qualified connector supplier base is small, with suppliers
including APH, TE, and ACES, with Lotes added subsequently.
Peer comparison
ACES’s primary local competitors in connectors include Lotes, Alltop, Argosy and JPC
Connectivity. In the cable industry, it competes with Bizlink and Longwell. ACES’s
foreign peers include Amphenol, TE and FIT.

Target price calculation
We initiate coverage of ACES with an O-PF rating and a target price of NT$270,
based on 20x 2H27-1H28SP EPS, supported by a strong 72% EPS Cagr in 2026-

28SP. We use a PE-based methodology for ACES, in line with other connector
companies. We initiate coverage with an Outperform rating.
Our target multiple of 20x is in line with the 2027SP average PE of 20.4x for Taiwan-
listed connector and cable peers with direct AI datacentre exposure, namely
BizLink, Lotes and JPC Connectivity. We view this as a reasonable anchor. ACES
offers faster earnings growth than the group, with 2027SP EPS growth of 121%

versus the peer average of 52%, while its 2027-28SP ROE of 21-24% sits at the low
end of the peer range of 22-52%.

We also believe the target PE premium is justified as the AI revenue contribution,
including AI data transmission and power, rises from 4% in 2024 to 48% in 2027SP.
The company maintains its key supplier position at Bloom as the company ramps up
its capacity expansion, while ASIC customer wins and higher connector content
from the Gen6 upgrade cycle should provide additional growth drivers. As the
company gradually shifts away from its legacy consumer electronics base and its

exposure to cyclical downturns, we see room for a structural rerating.

Source: SinoPac
Key risks
We see four key operational risks for ACES:
Raw material cost volatility: Copper prices drive a large share of Cogs for ACES's
cable products, while connectors carry far less exposure. ACES currently reprices
its cable products with customers on a quarterly basis, which has allowed it to

sustain a long-term GM of around 20% on power cables. However, the pass-through
might be lagging, so sharp copper price swings could cause quarterly GM to
fluctuate before repricing impact catches up.
Delays in ODM pull-in: ACES ships its high-speed connectors directly to ODM
customers. Because rack assembly sits at the final stage of the AI server supply
chain, any upstream disruption, such as components shortages and assembly yield
issue, could push out rack shipment schedules or reduce volumes. This could in turn

lead ODMs to cut or defer orders for high-speed connectors from ACES, creating
quarter-to-quarter volatility in server revenue.
Slower-than-expected ramp-up at Bloom Energy: Power cables for Bloom will
account for 28%/35% of ACES's revenue in 2027/28SP, which makes Bloom's
ramp-up the single largest swing factor in our forecasts. Delays could arise on both
the supply and demand sides. On the supply side, Bloom could fall behind its
capacity plan if new facility fit-out, equipment installation or hiring takes longer

than expected. On the demand side, its datacentre customers could defer
deployments due to grid interconnection, gas supply or financing hurdles, or due to
regulatory headwinds such as tighter permitting and emissions rules for on-site gas-
fired generation, utility restrictions on behind-the-meter power, or changes to fuel
cell tax incentives. Either scenario would directly reduce ACES's power cable
shipments. As volumes scale, Bloom may also qualify a second cable supplier, which

would add pressure to ACES's share and ASP.
Prolonged NB weakness: NB and Consumer remains ACES's largest segment at 35%
of 2026SP revenue, and our forecasts assume it returns to 4%/12% growth in
2027/28SP on a Windows 12-driven replacement cycle. If end-demand stays soft
or the replacement cycle slips, a further NB decline could offset part of the growth
from Bloom and AI servers and dilute group GM.

### Page 31

Wired for growth ACES - O-PF
6 October 2026 31
Investment thesis
ACES is evolving from a notebook connector maker into a supplier of AI
interconnects and power cables, with two growth engines ramping at the same
time. The first is Bloom Energy. ACES has co-developed SOFC power cables with
Bloom for about six years, and it is currently one of three suppliers of Bloom's power
cable assemblies, with around one-third of the total. It ships inner PCS and hot-box

cables in 2026 and adds external DLO high-voltage cables from 2027, with order
visibility extending to 2028 and beyond. The second engine is AI/ASIC servers. On
ASIC platforms, ODMs have more say in supplier selection, so ACES wins both
motherboard and daughterboard sockets, which lifts content from 10-20 to ~30
units per rack. Gen6 raises connector ASP by ~15%, and a new in-chassis PCIe Gen6
cable line, qualified by two CSPs, adds a higher-content stream. ACES integrates

vertically from wire drawing to final assembly, which makes it one of the few
Taiwanese suppliers able to ship connector-plus-cable solutions as CSPs
consolidate their vendor base.
Catalysts
(1) Bloom's capacity ramp from 2GW to 4GW/6GW in 2026-28 and new data-
center wins (Oracle, Nebius, AEP, Equinix) converting into cable orders; (2) DLO
high-voltage cable shipments starting in 2027; (3) ASIC customer ramps in 2H26
and a broader product range beyond MCIO into Multi-Track, board-to-board and

E1/E3 connectors; (4) rising Gen6 penetration on AI server trays; (5) high-speed
cable lines expanding from two to eight by end-2026, with GM improving from
~20% toward ~30%.
Valuation details
We adopt a PE-based valuation methodology as we believe the market will mainly
focus on the earnings growth outlooks of high-speed connector makers, which is
better reflected with PE metrics. We assign a target multiple of 20x PE, more than
+1sd of the three-year average, to reflect a strong outlook with margin expansion

and an EPS Cagr of 72% in 2026-28 on solid Bloom Energy demand and increasing
high speed connector content.
Investment risks
There could be downside risk to sales growth and margins if: (1) Bloom's
deployments or backlog conversion slow on financing, gas supply or permitting
delays, or ACES loses share among Bloom's cable suppliers; (2) ASIC programs ramp
more slowly or CSP Capex is cut; (3) Gen6 adoption is slower than expected, or
competition from Amphenol, TE, Molex and Lotes pressures ASP; (4) high-speed

cable yield and GM improve more slowly than we assume; (5) NB and consumer
demand weakens further on component shortages, tariffs and inflation; (6) TWD
appreciation or higher copper prices squeeze margins.

## Document 207: Compelling risk-reward in US 10-year bonds

Source PDF: all report pdf/Compelling risk-reward in US 10-year bonds.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-09-28
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Compelling risk-reward in 
US 10-year bonds  
Summary 
Event: The US 10 -year government bond yield has surged 
past 5.0% to a 19-year high and has now entered our three -
month forecast range of 5.0-5.25%. At the time of writing, the 
10-year yield is trading at 5.2%, its highest since June 2007’s 
high of 5.32%. The next resistance levels are the 2002 high 
of 5.47% and the 2001 high of 5.53%.  
We view the risk/reward for the US 10-year government 
bond after the recent yield spike as  compelling and we 
are initiating a long opportunistic idea at the current 
yield. As the table on the right shows, if the 10 -year yield 
rises to 6%, investors would face less than 1% in negative 
total returns over a one-year period, whereas if the yield falls 
below 5.0%, they would gain at least 6% in total returns.  
While we continue to prefer the 3-7-year bond maturity range 
for income generation (the US 5-year bond yield is also 
above 5%), and broadly prefer corporate over government 
bonds amid solid corporate fundamentals, the US 10 -year 
government bond yield has finally reached reasonable levels 
for investors looking for slightly longer maturities. 
Rationale: We expect the US 10-year government bond yield 
to ease to 4.75-5.0% over a 12-month period due to: 
Easing oil prices and inflation over next 12 months: While 
the yield is likely to remain elevated in the next three months 
due to elevated oil prices caused by geopolitical uncertainty, 
heavy AI investment-driven bond issuance and a resilient US 
economy, it is likely to ease towards the 4.75 -5.0% range 
over 12  months as oil prices moderate either due to a 
resolution in the Middle East or a rerouting of Middle East 
energy supplies and as the impact of tariffs fade.  
We expect s hallower Fed rate hikes than market s: We 
expect a total of 50bps of Fed rate hikes by June 2027, before 
a cut in H2 as US inflation eventually eases towards the Fed’s 
2% target. This  rate trajectory is shallower than c. 95bps of 
further hikes priced by markets over the next 12 months. 
 The benchmark US 10-year government bond yield has 
risen to its highest since 2007, offering medium-term 
investors a compelling risk-reward trade-off 
US 10-year government bond yield and previous highs

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 28 September 2026

Investor positioning adjustment: In the very near-term, the 
US 10 -year bond yield has room to move lower as hedge 
funds using systematic strategies (CTAs) are extremely short 
US government bonds, raising the chance of short -covering 
if upcoming jobs and inflation data miss estimates. 
A shallow Fed hiking cycle  
We expect the shallowest Fed hiking cycle in almost 
three decades. This should enable the US 10-year yield to 
ease towards 4.75 -5.0% over a 12 -month period. Following 
this month’s increase, we forecast two further 25bp hikes by 
June 2027, followed by a cut in H2 2027. Markets price at 
least three more hikes by June and a strong probability of a 
fourth by September. Our projected 75bp s of total hikes this 
cycle compares with 525bps of hikes in 2022–23, 225bps in 
2015–18, 425bps in 2004–06 and 175bp in 1999–2000. The 
last smaller mid-cycle move was a single 25bps hike in 1997. 
Why we expect a more cautious Fed 
The latest hike restored the Fed’s credibility after renewed 
oil-price pressures, elevated inflation and a balanced labour 
market prompted a unanimous policy vote. However, the 
following factors argue against aggressive tightening: 
Supply-driven inflation: Energy costs and AI-infrastructure 
constraints, rather than excess demand, are driving prices. 
Higher rates cannot repair supply and may weaken growth. 
Labour is no longer the main inflation source:  Labour 
supply and demand are better balanced, wage growth has 
moderated and unemployment is near full employment. AI 
further weakens the traditional Phillips Curve relationship 
between unemployment and inflation. 
Disinflation should resume:  Energy pressures may fade 
after the US mid-term elections, while the one-off inflationary 
effects of tariffs should diminish after 2025–26. 
AI raises the neutral rate , but dampens inflation:  
Investment and productivity may lift the neutral rate ( r*), the 
rate consistent with stable inflation and full employment, 
while efficiency gains limit wage-price spirals. 
Markets are already tightening  financial conditions : 
Higher bond yields have raised borrowing costs and 
restrained activity, doing part of the Fed’s work. 
The cycle began from a higher base:  The Fed funds rate 
started at 3.75%, versus 0.25% in the previous two cycles 
and 1% before that, reducing additional tightening required. 
Monetary transmission has weakened:  Wealthier 
households drive more spending but are less rate -sensitive, 
while lower -income consumers are already constrained. 
Further hikes may , therefore, curb demand less effectively 
while causing disproportionate harm to low-income families. 
 We expect a shallower Fed rate hiking cycle compared 
with market expectations

### Page 3

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 28 September 2026

Small business conditions are deteriorating:  Higher 
financing and energy costs are weakening sentiment, as 
reflected in the latest Beige Book . The renewed 
underperformance of US small capitalisation (S&P 600) and 
mid capitalisation (S&P 400) equity indices  versus the large 
cap S&P 500 index over the past couple of months also show 
rising pressure on small businesses. 
Fiscal and financial -stability risks are rising:  Aggressive 
Fed tightening would increase US debt-servicing costs as the 
US Treasury seeks cheaper funding through shorter-maturity 
issuance, while potentially amplifying financial-system stress. 
Upside risk to bond yields 
Our view of lower bond yields carries the following risks: 
Persistent inflation:  Repeated supply shocks, strong AI -
related investment demand and tighter immigration controls 
could keep inflation above target and force steeper Fed rate 
hikes. With unemployment near the Fed’s estimate of full 
employment, a sharp drop in labour supply could intensify 
wage pressures and destabilise inflation expectations . We 
note that long-term expectations remain contained for now. 
Geopolitical escalation:  An intensification of geopolitical 
conflicts after the mid -term elections could lift energy prices 
and inflation. However, a Democrat -controlled or divided 
Congress may constrain US foreign-policy initiatives through 
tighter budgets, while Republicans could turn less supportive 
as attention shifts to the 2028 presidential race. 
Higher neutral rate: AI investments could raise the neutral 
rate (r*), turning current monetary policy settings 
accommodative. This could necessitate further rate hikes. 
However, the Fed has barely raised its nominal neutral rate 
estimate, lifting it to 3.2% in September from 3.0% a year ago. 
Bottomline: The outlook for rates remains geopolitics 
dependent, with all Fed meetings live . However, based on 
our core scenario, we see the US 10-year yield settling lower 
in the 4.75-5.0% in 6-12 months. A shallow Fed hiking cycle 
due to expected disinflation offers investors a reasonably 
attractive risk-reward in US 10-year government bonds. 
US 30-year government bonds , on the other hand, may 
continue to see elevated yields as the US fiscal deficit is 
unlikely to have improved next year, and may look much 
worse. As a result, “term premium” may keep the ultra -long 
end elevated. Short of some unorthodox policy to artifici ally 
cap them lower, the 30-year yield is likely to continue to drift 
higher towards the 5.5-5.75% range into H2 2027. 
— Jonathan Liang, CIO for Fixed Income and FX   
Rajat Bhattacharya, Senior Investment Strategist 
Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist

## Document 226: EcoNews of 5 October 2026 (2026-10-05)

Source PDF: all report pdf/EcoNews of 5 October 2026 (2026-10-05).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-05
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
5
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.36 / 5 October 2026ECONEWS
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
ADVANCED ECONOMIES
EUROPEAN UNION / EUROZONE
Resilient corporate sentiment, upside inflation surprises. The Euro -
pean Commission’s Economic Sentiment Index for September showed
continued recovery in industry (+1.2 pts to -3.8, a 3.5 year high), with
a clear rebound in production and employment expectations, while

services improved further (+0.5 pt to 6.1). By contrast, household
confidence and sentiment in construction weakened further, as did
the composite index. These surveys broadly corroborate the PMIs,
with the September manufacturing PMI revised up by 0.2 pt to 52.9.
The unemployment rate held steady at a low 6.4%. Meanwhile, the
September flash inflation estimate came in above consensus at 3.8%.
The increase was driven mainly by energy (18.8% y/y, +4.5 pp) and

services (3.2%, +0.2 pp). Non-energy industrial goods inflation eased
slightly to 1.1% y/y, but the trend is firming: the 3m/3m annualised
rate is at a three-year high of 2.5%. Speaking before the data release,
ECB President Lagarde argued for a “measured response” and noted
that the increase in bond yields was delivering some of the needed
restraint. Tightening is also occurring via the cost-of-borrowing for
mortgage loans, which increased in August for the fifth consecutive

month to 3.6% (+6 bp m/m), a level unseen since September 2024.
It was unchanged for loans to non-financial corporations (3.8%).
Upcoming: ECB minutes (Thursday) and EU Trade Commissioner
Šefcovic's visit to Beijing (Thursday-Friday)
France. In the eye of the bond market storm. The government’s draft
budget for 2027, despite targeting significant adjustment (see our
detailed analysis), failed to stop the surge in the OAT yield, which
passed 4.9% on Friday morning (a high unseen since 2002), while the

OAT-bund spread reached a peak of 151 bps before easing somewhat
along with the global bond market selloff. (The peak coincided with
a statement by Bundesbank President Nagel that the ECB’s instru -
ments are focused on monetary policy transmission, not any spread
level). As expected, the draft budget law targets a deficit of 5% of GDP
(down from 5.4% in 2026), implying EUR 54 bn of savings, of which
about 2/3 coming from reduced spending. France’s independent fiscal

watchdog commended the spending moderation effort but pointed out
optimistic economic assumptions.
Meanwhile, inflation surprised on the upside: HICP inflation accele -
rated to 3.4% y/y in September (consensus: 3.1%), from 2.6% in August.
The sharp rise in energy prices (21.2%) was amplified by a 10% y/y
increase in the price of fresh food (linked to the summer drought).
Core inflation rose to 1.6% y/y in September, up from 1.3% in August
(services inflation at 2.2%; +0.3 pp m/m). The rise in industrial pro -

ducer prices accelerated to 6% y/y in August from 4.3% in July (+67%
versus +50% for refined products). Higher fuel prices also weighed on
household consumption, which fell by -0.5% m/m in August. On the
brighter side, the September manufacturing PMI was revised up by
0.3 points to 50.6.
Germany. Inflation accelerates to 3-year high. HICP inflation reached
3.3% y/y in September (vs. 2.9% in August and 3.2% expected) ac -
cording to the flash estimate (the highest since December 2023),

driven by energy prices. Core inflation rose by 2.4% (same as in Au -
gust) and services inflation edged down to 2.7% (vs. 2.8% in August).
The unemployment rate was stable in September at 6.4%.
Upcoming: August factory orders (Tuesday) and industrial production
(Wednesday)
Italy and Spain. Upside inflation surprises, business sentiment gains.
In Italy, HICP rose by 4.1% (vs. 3.2% in August), while core rose by 1.7%
(vs. 1.5% in August). In Spain, HICP inflation accelerated to 5% y/y

(vs. 4.6% in August), while core inflation reached 3.1% y/y (vs. 2.9%
earlier). Business sentiment, measured by the European Commission’s
ESI, rose slightly in both manufacturing and services in Italy, and
more significantly in Spain, where unlike in the rest of Europe, consu -
mer sentiment improved as well. Less positively, Italy’s unemploy -
ment rate unexpectedly rose to 6.2%.
UNITED KINGDOM
Bold new policy paths and upward growth revision. Prime Minister

Burnham broke several policy taboos in his Labour Party Conference
Speech, notably reconsidering Brexit and abandoning the so-called
“triple lock” guaranteeing that state pensions rise each year by the
highest of inflation, wages, or 2.5%. These proposals would be taken
up in the next Parliament. He also vowed to dismantle the legacy
of Thatcherism in public services and utilities. Real GDP growth for
Q2 2026 was revised upward to 0.5% q/q (vs. 0.4% previously, fol -

lowing 0.6% in Q1), driven by a stronger-than-expected performance
in trade and investment. Inflation expectations in the Bank of En -
gland’s Decision Maker Panel rose to 3.9% for own prices and 3.3%
for CPI one year ahead.
UNITED STATES
Solid growth, underwhelming payrolls. Q2 GDP growth was revised
up to 2.2% annualised (from 1.5%), with broad-based revisions to in -
vestment and consumption. The September ISM manufacturing in -
dex edged down (-0.1 pt to 54.5) on a drop in production. However,

new orders, a leading indicator, improved, and the employment in -
dex held at its highest level in four years. Payrolls rose by 29,000
in September, well below expectations, while the two months prior
were revised down by a combined 60,000. Job gains in healthcare
(+23k) and leisure/hospitality (+10k) were partly offset by losses in
Government (-10k). The unemployment rate rose to 4.2% (+0.1pp)
on higher labour force participation. Private hourly earnings growth

continued to slow (3% y/y, lowest since mid-2021). The August per -
sonal consumption expenditure (PCE) inflation report surprised to
the downside (3.4% vs. 3.7% expected), and core PCE was unchanged
at 3%. On the household side, the saving rate fell sharply (4.1% of
disposable income in August, from 4.6% in July), explaining the unex -
pected resilience of consumption (+0.6% in August inflation-adjusted).
All in all, this suggests the Fed will continue to raise interest rates

but doesn’t need to rush. We expect the next 25 bps hike in December.
Upcoming: September ISM services (Monday); FOMC Minutes (We -
dnesday).
JAPAN
Booming business sentiment. The Tankan survey index of large ma -
nufacturers rose to +24 (+2 pts q/q), while small and medium en -
terprises saw significant gains (+14; +5 pts q/q and +23; +5 pts q/q
respectively), driven by demand for semiconductors and production
equipment. Services sentiment weakened slightly (+35; -2 pts q/q for

large firms). Investment intentions remain strong among large cor -
porations and are showing signs of recovery in SMEs.

### Page 2

6
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.36 / 5 October 2026
The bank
for a changing

ECONEWS Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
EMERGING ECONOMIES
Manufacturing PMIs generally stronger. The index rose to 56.7 in
Taiwan (the highest level since 2021) and 53.9 in South Korea, boosted
by strong global demand for AI goods. Korean exports increased by
a record 83.5% y/y in September (of which chips: +263% y/y), mostly
led by export prices. The manufacturing PMI also improved and was

comfortably above 50 in India, Indonesia and Thailand. It declined
slightly in Vietnam (to 51.9). Malaysia and the Philippines stood out
with PMIs falling slightly below 50. PMIs also generally improved
in central Europe. In Latin America, PMIs were more mixed: falling
sharply in Brazil to 44.8, its lowest level since early 2023, but slightly
improving in Mexico (to 50) and Colombia (to 54.6).
ASIA
China. Better outlook on small stimulus and higher PMIs. Following

a string of data showing too sluggish domestic demand to meet the
Government’s growth target of 4.5 to 5%, the State Council announced
a package of stimulus measures, including the expansion of some re -
lending facilities aimed at financing tech/innovation, agriculture and
SMEs; new spending to be financed by carried-over local govern -
ment debt quotas; and a small interest rate subsidy on mortgages.
The latest PMIs published by the NBS and by RatingDog improved

marginally, with both now showing expansion in both manufacturing
and services.
EUROPE, MIDDLE EAST
Poland. Strong uptick in September’s inflation reading. Inflation
came in at 4.0% y/y in September (the highest level since June
2025) after 3.4% y/y in August, driven by higher prices at the pump.
The increase led the government to lower its VAT rate on fuel to 8%
(23% previously) from October until year-end, which should help ease
inflationary pressures. Overall, inflation may not significantly exceed

the Central bank’s target on average in 2026. The Central Bank may
nonetheless consider one rate hike by year end.
Türkiye. A financial scandal with limited macro impact. A Ponzi sche-
me collapse has impacted around 455k retail investors with assets
reportedly worth about 1% of GDP . The crisis was swiftly handled
by the authorities to prevent financial contagion. 131 funds and two
home-finance institutions involved in the scandal were suspended

and taken over by public banks for orderly and fair disposal of their
assets. Three investment banks and 2 factoring companies were
transferred to the Savings Deposit Insurance Fund. So far, 217 people
have been subjected to legal action, of whom 90 have been jailed
pending trial. Since September 9, the BIST-30 index lost 10% but is
still 25% higher than at end-2025, and foreign investors bought equi -
ties at the highest pace in 3 months last week, suggesting a buy-the-

dip reading of the situation. There has been no or marginal spillover
effects on the exchange rate, domestic interest rates and sovereign
spreads. At this stage, contagion risk to the banking sector is very low
but the damage to small investors may have negative spillovers on
consumption through negative wealth effect. Separately, the manu -
facturing PMI fell further in September to 47.9, signaling deepening
contraction.
Saudi Arabia. Budget deficit jumps on cost of war. Budget deficit is

now forecasted at USD 65.4 bn this year (-4.9% of GDP), up from
USD 44.3 bn (-3.3% of GDP) in the initial financing bill. Revenue is
expected to be slightly higher (+3.7%) as high oil prices more than
offset the decline in oil export volume. But spending has been revised
up by 9.3%. The new growth projection is also bearish, with GDP
expected to contract by -3.6% due to the decline in oil activity while
consensus sees economy growing by +0.2%.
LATIN AMERICA

Brazil. A tight runoff, a tough policy mix inheritance. Senator Flávio
Bolsonaro and Brazil’s incumbent President Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva
will face off in a runoff on October 25 th after neither won a majority
in Sunday’s general election (47% vs. 45% of the vote respectively).
Since Brazil returned to democracy in 1989, no candidate who trailed
in the first round has won the runoff. The next President will inherit
a slowing economy and a difficult policy mix: pressing ahead with

fiscal consolidation while normalising monetary policy amid recur -
ring supply shocks (adverse weather conditions, El Niño, war in the
Middle East).
ENERGY
Energy. Limited relief, electricity excepted. Re-escalation risks ap -
pear to be rising in the very short term in the Gulf and the Red Sea.
At the same time, the record high level of Gulf oil exports in Sep -
tember (above 80% of pre-war level including bypass and trade shutt-
le) has been enough to stabilize prices at a high level, albeit not to

put significant and lasting downward pressure on crude prices.
- Crude oil: prices declined slightly over the week but remain high
(-3.5% w/w to USD 101.6/b on Monday morning for Brent). The gap
with physical barrel prices (Dated Brent) is rising again USD 24.4/b on
Friday), signalling persisting tensions on the physical market.
- The diesel market got some relief from a G7 decision late on Friday
to release 100 mb of oil and diesel emergency stocks, focused on the

latter. It should take place over the next four months and reportedly
puts off the table US export restrictions. The European benchmark
fell markedly (-12% w/w to USD80/b on Monday morning). However,
market pressure could rise again soon as most Chinese refiners an -
nounced the suspension of fuel exports for October as the government
want to prioritize domestic supply security.
- EU wholesale electricity prices rose sharply in September, reaching

EUR 146/MWh on average, the highest level since Dec 2022.

## Document 236: Eurozone_ Improving business sentiment, driven by tech, is spreading to other sectors (2026-10-14)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Eurozone_ Improving business sentiment, driven by tech, is spreading to other sectors (2026-10-14).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

14 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
EUROZONE: IMPROVING BUSINESS SENTIMENT, DRIVEN BY TECH, IS SPREADING TO OTHER SECTORS
Stéphane Colliac & Tarik Rharrab
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
The September PMI surveys confirm that growth in the Eurozone is getting stronger, boosted in particular by new export orders, which are
still rising. Sectors that were already performing well – technology and capital goods (including for defence) – are continuing to grow, while

those that had been struggling until now – aeronautics, consumer goods and financial services – are seeing their production indices recover.
This strong performance is particularly noteworthy, given that it has not yet been undermined by higher inflation.
The heatmap shows the PMI index readings: a reading above 50 indicates expansion, while a reading below 50 signals contraction compared to the previous
month. For each indicator, sectors are ranked in descending order based on their September 2026 reading.

Shades of red represent values above 50, while shades of blue represent values below 50: the intensity of the colour increases the further away they are
from this threshold.
Production growth spreads to more sectors
The PMI surveys on business sentiment confirm that business activity in the Eurozone is expanding at a faster pace and spreading to more sectors.
Household-consumption-related sectors (goods and services), which were adversely affected in the spring by the outbreak of the conflict in the Middle

East, do not appear to be negatively impacted by the rising inflation observed since August. The aeronautics sector, where production has mainly been
limited by supply constraints, is also showing signs of improvement.
SOURCE: S&P GLOBAL, BNP PARIBAS

These trends, although still moderate, come alongside a drive to upgrade equipment, fuelled by investment in digital technology, electrification
(which is supporting the automotive sector) and defence. The momentum in technology goods and services further strengthened in September.
The production index for computer and electronics hit a five-year high, while the software services production index stayed close to its July peak.

Financial services are also enjoying a significant improvement, reflecting the healthy lending momentum despite interest rates starting to rise
(see our analysis).
Growth driven by exports
Exports drove growth in the first half of 2026, both across the Eurozone as a whole (see our analysis) and among its key members (notably
Germany). This momentum is also increasing, as indicated by the ‘new export orders’ component of the various PMI indices. As was the case with

production, there has been a particularly noteworthy increase for capital goods (technology and machinery) and related services, particularly digital
services. This trend is playing out more broadly, as, in September, new export orders rose across all sub-sectors, with the exception of intermediate
goods and consumer goods, where contraction recently eased. There has been a significant improvement in the transport sector, particularly in
the aeronautics industry, which has major markets in Asia. Recent data on French exports show that aeronautics exports to this region are rising

once again.
The convergence between rising production and increasing export-order backlogs shows that Eurozone growth is based on an increasingly solid
foundation. Inflationary pressures are not holding it back for the time being.
